research and study how to best implement this ticket, don't make a plan yet: Work on Linear issue ABA-115:

<issue identifier="ABA-115">
<title>/stock:model report — glidepath + scenario fan visuals</title>
<description>
## Problem

After <issue id="89e1f4f4-8b04-4c5b-9931-7f4f837de45f">ABA-110</issue> (SBC (Stock-Based Compensation) strip) and <issue id="e78e36cd-49f1-441f-96a9-317acc594230">ABA-111</issue> (growth-rate cap) landed, `/stock:model` now produces honest but **stark-looking** scenario outputs. Example META 2026-05-17:

* Bear IV (Intrinsic Value): $184
* Base IV: $393
* Bull IV: $778
* Current price: $614

Reading the base case in isolation ("$393 vs $614 → 36% overvalued") is misleading without the trajectory context. The base case mechanically applies the consensus growth ceiling in Y2 (META: 52% trailing → 18% capped), which looks like a cliff but is a smoothing artifact of the spec. A reader anchored on "base = realistic central case" will misread it as "the model thinks META is 36% overvalued" when the actual message is "the market is pricing META as a bull case — here's what has to keep going right."

## Why now

Thesis construction depends on **explaining how the numbers got where they got** — more than the numbers themselves. Without visuals, the reader has to do the unpacking in their head; with visuals, the trajectory and the asymmetry are immediately legible.

User quote (2026-05-17 session): *"reading a 'base case' that's so far from the current stock price has some context — it's unlikely that a company that has kept massive momentum in growth will suddenly stop, so this is important to put into context."*

## Scope

Add three visuals to the report UI for ESTABLISHED-path Model output:

1. **CAGR (Compound Annual Growth Rate) glidepath** — line chart showing trailing-3y CAGR (clean, SBC-stripped), then Y1→Y5 projected CAGR for each scenario (bear/base/bull). Annotate cap point ("capped from 52.1% by 18% fallback ceiling" for the base line).
2. **FCF (Free Cash Flow) margin trajectory** — area chart showing 3y historical reported vs clean FCF margin, with a constant-margin extension for Y1→Y5. Visually surfaces that base case assumes flat-line margins (no operating leverage) — and invites the user to consider whether they buy that assumption.
3. **Scenario fan chart** — cone/fan plot of Y1→Y5 FCF projection for bear/base/bull, with the corresponding terminal-year discounted-to-PV (Present Value) markers, and the current price line for reference.

## Acceptance criteria

1. UI renders the three visuals for any covered ticker's latest `reports/TICKER_YYYYMMDD.json` where `stages.model` is present.
2. Cap-point annotation reads from `stages.model.growth_rate` block (added in <issue id="e78e36cd-49f1-441f-96a9-317acc594230">ABA-111</issue>) — specifically `trailing_3y_cagr`, `applied_base_cagr`, `cap_source`.
3. FCF margin chart reads clean and reported series from `stages.model.fcf_margin_ttm` (clean) and `stages.model.fcf_margin_ttm_reported` (reported, added in <issue id="89e1f4f4-8b04-4c5b-9931-7f4f837de45f">ABA-110</issue>).
4. Scenario fan chart sources from `stages.model.scenarios.{bear,base,bull}` (existing).
5. Each visual has a one-sentence caption explaining the assumption it surfaces (not the chart mechanics — the *thesis* implication).
6. Pre-profit variant either gets its own analogous visuals or is explicitly scoped out (decide during design).

## Out of scope

* Glidepath shaping (introducing year-by-year CAGR taper instead of flat Y2-Y5) — separate ticket if worth doing
* Adding a margin-expansion lever to *base* case — separate methodology ticket if worth doing
* Sensitivity heatmap (WACC × g) — separate ticket

## Related

* Builds on <issue id="89e1f4f4-8b04-4c5b-9931-7f4f837de45f">ABA-110</issue> (SBC strip surfaces clean vs reported margins in JSON)
* Builds on <issue id="e78e36cd-49f1-441f-96a9-317acc594230">ABA-111</issue> (growth-rate block surfaces cap mechanics in JSON)
* Likely lives in Wave 4 (UI/report polish) of M5; defer until playbook layer (<issue id="70664e55-f843-4fed-8462-d8e6e78141cb">ABA-112</issue>) lands so visuals reference playbook narrative

## Methodology footnote (for the reader)

The base case is intentionally a **"stop overdelivering"** anchor:

* Margins flat at today's clean FCF margin (no operating-leverage assumption)
* Growth decelerates from trailing print to consensus-5y ceiling (or 18% fallback) in Y2
* Discount rate at WACC (Weighted Average Cost of Capital) baseline

The bull case is where margin-expansion + sustained-growth assumptions live. This is by design — it forces the thesis to articulate which levers it's betting on, rather than baking optimism into the central case.
</description>
<team name="Personal"/>

1	import { useMemo, useState } from 'react';
2	import { loadReports } from './lib/reports.js';
3	import ScreenReport from './components/ScreenReport.jsx';
4	
5	const TABS = ['Screen', 'Signal', 'Model', 'Timing', 'Summary'];
6	
7	export default function App() {
8	  const reports = useMemo(() => loadReports(), []);
9	  const [selectedPath, setSelectedPath] = useState(reports[0]?.path ?? null);
10	  const [tab, setTab] = useState('Screen');
11	
12	  const current = reports.find((r) => r.path === selectedPath) ?? reports[0];
13	
14	  if (!current) {
15	    return (
16	      <div className="app">
17	        <h1>Equity Research</h1>
18	        <p className="empty">No reports found in <code>reports/</code>.</p>
19	      </div>
20	    );
21	  }
22	
23	  const { data } = current;
24	  const stages = data.stages ?? {};
25	
26	  return (
27	    <div className="app">
28	      <div className="header">
29	        <h1>{data.ticker}{data.company ? ` — ${data.company}` : ''}</h1>
30	        <span className="date">{data.date}</span>
31	        <select
32	          className="picker"
33	          name="report"
34	          aria-label="Select report"
35	          value={current.path}
36	          onChange={(e) => setSelectedPath(e.target.value)}
37	        >
38	          {reports.map((r) => (
39	            <option key={r.path} value={r.path}>
40	              {r.ticker} — {r.dateStr}
41	            </option>
42	          ))}
43	        </select>
44	      </div>
45	
46	      <div className="tabs">
47	        {TABS.map((name) => {
48	          const key = name.toLowerCase();
49	          const hasData = key === 'summary' ? true : Boolean(stages[key]);
50	          return (
51	            <button
52	              key={name}
53	              className={`tab ${tab === name ? 'active' : ''}`}
54	              onClick={() => setTab(name)}
55	              disabled={!hasData}
56	            >
57	              {name}
58	            </button>
59	          );
60	        })}
61	      </div>
62	
63	      {tab === 'Screen' && <ScreenReport stage={stages.screen} />}
64	      {tab !== 'Screen' && <p className="empty">{tab} view not yet implemented.</p>}
65	    </div>
66	  );
67	}
68

1	import { verdictClass, ratioLabel, formatRatio } from '../lib/formatters.js';
2	
3	export default function ScreenReport({ stage }) {
4	  if (!stage) {
5	    return <p className="empty">No screen data in this report.</p>;
6	  }
7	
8	  const { verdict, profit_stage, ratios = {}, rationale } = stage;
9	  const badgeClass = `badge ${verdictClass(verdict)}`;
10	
11	  return (
12	    <div className="card">
13	      <h2>
14	        Screen
15	        <span className={badgeClass}>{verdict ?? 'UNKNOWN'}</span>
16	        {profit_stage && <span style={{ color: '#777', fontWeight: 400, fontSize: '0.85rem' }}>
17	          {profit_stage}
18	        </span>}
19	      </h2>
20	
21	      <div className="ratios">
22	        {Object.entries(ratios).map(([key, value]) => (
23	          <div key={key} className="ratio">
24	            <div className="label">{ratioLabel(key)}</div>
25	            <div className="value">{formatRatio(value)}</div>
26	          </div>
27	        ))}
28	      </div>
29	
30	      {rationale && <p className="rationale">{rationale}</p>}
31	    </div>
32	  );
33	}
34

1	const modules = import.meta.glob('../../../reports/*.json', { eager: true });
2	
3	function parseFilename(path) {
4	  const file = path.split('/').pop().replace(/\.json$/, '');
5	  const [ticker, dateStr] = file.split('_');
6	  return { ticker, dateStr };
7	}
8	
9	export function loadReports() {
10	  const reports = Object.entries(modules).map(([path, mod]) => {
11	    const { ticker, dateStr } = parseFilename(path);
12	    return { path, ticker, dateStr, data: mod.default ?? mod };
13	  });
14	  reports.sort((a, b) => b.dateStr.localeCompare(a.dateStr) || b.ticker.localeCompare(a.ticker));
15	  return reports;
16	}
17	
```

1	export function verdictClass(verdict) {
2	  switch ((verdict || '').toUpperCase()) {
3	    case 'PASS':
4	    case 'BUY':
5	    case 'ACT NOW':
6	      return 'pass';
7	    case 'WATCH':
8	    case 'CONDITIONAL':
9	    case 'WAIT FOR CATALYST':
10	      return 'watch';
11	    case 'SKIP':
12	    case 'CAUTION':
13	    case 'WAIT FOR BETTER ENTRY':
14	      return 'skip';
15	    default:
16	      return 'unknown';
17	  }
18	}
19	
20	const RATIO_LABELS = {
21	  pe_ratio: 'P/E',
22	  ps_ratio: 'P/S',
23	  ev_ebitda: 'EV/EBITDA',
24	  pfcf: 'P/FCF',
25	  ev_revenue: 'EV/Revenue'
26	};
27	
28	export function ratioLabel(key) {
29	  return RATIO_LABELS[key] ?? key;
30	}
31	
32	export function formatRatio(value) {
33	  if (value == null || Number.isNaN(value)) return '—';
34	  return Number(value).toFixed(1);
35	}
36

```
300	
301	When `status = "applied"`, COMPUTE Step 2 (Year-1 FCF anchor) uses `base_anchor_multiplier` from this sub-section on the **base** scenario only. The output cap (Step 6b in COMPUTE) may further clamp the multiplier; `clamped_from` records the pre-clamp value. When `status ∈ {unavailable, no_kpi_mapping, user_skipped, direction_disagreement}`, `base_anchor_multiplier = 1.00` and COMPUTE Step 2b is a no-op.
302	
303	### COMPUTE — Two-stage DCF
304	
305	Execute in order. Show the working — every scenario must be reproducible from the printed assumptions.
306	
307	**Step 1 — Derive trailing FCF base and margin (SBC-stripped per ABA-110).**
308	
309	yfinance's OCF adds SBC back as a non-cash charge, so reported `free_cash_flow` silently treats stock-based compensation as free. `/stock-signal` already strips SBC from EPS (Step 5); the DCF base must do the same or the two skills disagree on the same company. Strip first, then derive margin and CAGR.
310	
311	- `sbc_ttm` = `years[0].stock_based_compensation` (refuse via the GATHER null-handler above if null — never substitute, never assume zero).
312	- `fcf_ttm_reported` = `years[0].free_cash_flow` (latest reported FY; if quarterly TTM is unavailable, accept the latest annual figure). Held for the audit trail and the OUTPUT block; **not** used as the DCF anchor.
313	- `fcf_ttm` = `fcf_ttm_reported − sbc_ttm` (the SBC-stripped, "clean" FCF — this is the DCF base).
314	- `fcf_margin_ttm_reported` = `fcf_ttm_reported / years[0].revenue` (audit-trail only; surfaces in OUTPUT side-by-side with the clean margin).
315	- `fcf_margin_ttm` = `fcf_ttm / years[0].revenue` (the **clean** SBC-stripped margin — this is the margin that anchors the Y1 FCF projection in Step 2).
316	- `fcf_cagr_3y` is computed on **clean** FCF on both ends — never on reported. For each historical year used, derive `clean_fcf_y = years[y].free_cash_flow − years[y].stock_based_compensation`. Then `fcf_cagr_3y = (clean_fcf_y0 / clean_fcf_y3) ^ (1/3) − 1` when 4y of usable history is available; if only 3y is available use `^(1/2)` with the oldest available year as the base; if only 2y is available, set `fcf_cagr_3y = (clean_fcf_y0 / clean_fcf_yprior) − 1` and flag confidence = MEDIUM. State which formulation was used. If any historical-year `stock_based_compensation` is null, the SBC paste-in (GATHER null-handler) must cover the full required series — never silently fall back to reported-FCF CAGR for the missing year.
317	
318	**Step 2 — Year-1 FCF anchor under each scenario.**
319	
320	Year-1 FCF is anchored on NTM consensus, then perturbed per scenario.
321	
322	Determine the FCF margin anchor:
323	- When playbook provides `terminal_margin`: `fcf_margin_anchor = playbook.terminal_margin` (normalised steady-state margin; replaces TTM when capex cycles or one-time costs distort the trailing window). Surface in OUTPUT as `FCF margin anchor: X.X% (playbook override; TTM clean: Y.Y%)`.
324	- Otherwise: `fcf_margin_anchor = fcf_margin_ttm` (SBC-stripped TTM margin from Step 1).
325	
326	Compute `fcf_y1_consensus = ntm_revenue × fcf_margin_anchor` as the consensus anchor.
327	
328	| Scenario | Y1 FCF |
329	|---|---|
330	| Bear | `fcf_y1_consensus × 0.85` — revenue miss + 200 bps margin compression |
331	| Base | `fcf_y1_consensus × base_anchor_multiplier` — NTM consensus, perturbed by the engagement modifier (see Step 2b) |
332	| Bull | `fcf_y1_consensus × 1.10` — revenue beat + operating leverage |
333	
334	**Step 2b — Engagement modifier on base scenario (FR3/FR4, C3/C4/C5).**
335	
336	When the engagement-modifier GATHER sub-step emitted `status: "applied"`, the base scenario's Y1 FCF anchor is perturbed by `base_anchor_multiplier ∈ [0.96, 1.04]` from that sub-step. When `status ∈ {unavailable, no_kpi_mapping, user_skipped, direction_disagreement}` OR when the `--engagement-modifier` flag was absent, `base_anchor_multiplier = 1.00` and this step is a no-op (the base scenario's Y1 anchor equals `fcf_y1_consensus` unchanged).
337	
338	Bear and bull are **never** modifier-perturbed — scenario-axis independence (C5) and the range-integrity invariant `bear_iv < base_iv < bull_iv` both depend on the modifier touching base alone.
339	
340	**Output cap (C4 / FR4b / NFR4 — applied AFTER Step 6 produces base IV.)** Per the ADR, the modifier's effect on the base scenario's per-share intrinsic value is additionally capped at ≤5%. This cap is evaluated post-COMPUTE in **Step 6b** below (after the equity bridge produces `intrinsic_value_per_share`), because the input multiplier alone cannot predict the IV impact — DCF leverage means a 4% Y1 nudge can translate to anywhere from 2% to 9% on per-share IV depending on the WACC / terminal-growth regime.
341	
342	**Revision-direction agreement guardrail** (spike-decision §"Lead vs confirm", wired in GATHER Step 5b). When engagement direction and the trailing 30-day EPS revision direction strictly disagree, GATHER emits `status: "direction_disagreement"` with `base_anchor_multiplier = 1.00`, and this step is a no-op. When they agree (or revision data is unavailable — legacy fall-through), the modifier applies per C3/C4 and the audit trail records `agreement: true | null` respectively.
343	
344	**Step 3 — Years 2–5 FCF growth per scenario (sanity-capped per ABA-111).**
345	
346	Trailing 3y CAGR is a useful **floor** signal (the company actually grew this fast recently) but a poor **central case** when the trailing window includes a depressed base year — extrapolating that rate produces mechanical, not analytical, terminal FCFs (META FY22 was the Reality Labs / cost-reset trough; NVDA FY23 pre-dated the AI boom). Cap base-scenario growth against a consensus or fallback ceiling, then drive bear/bull off the capped base so scenario-axis independence is preserved.
347	
348	```
349	consensus_growth = get_estimates(ticker).eps_growth_5y     # long-term sell-side consensus; may be null
350	fallback_ceiling = 0.18                                     # 18% — historical mature-tech ceiling
351	playbook_ceiling = playbook.growth_ceiling if playbook.loaded else None   # ABA-112: playbook override
352	
353	# Priority: consensus (most current external data) > playbook ceiling > generic fallback
354	ceiling          = (consensus_growth if consensus_growth is not None
355	                   else (playbook_ceiling if playbook_ceiling is not None
356	                   else fallback_ceiling))
357	base_cagr        = min(fcf_cagr_3y, ceiling)
358	cap_applied      = (base_cagr < fcf_cagr_3y)
359	cap_source       = ("consensus_5y_growth"   if (consensus_growth is not None and cap_applied)
360	                   else "playbook"          if (playbook_ceiling is not None and cap_applied)
361	                   else "fallback_ceiling"  if cap_applied
362	                   else None)
363	```
364	
365	The cap only narrows growth — it never raises it. If `fcf_cagr_3y` is already at or below the ceiling, `base_cagr = fcf_cagr_3y` and `cap_applied = false` (e.g. GOOG, whose trailing FCF CAGR runs near consensus — the cap does not fire and the output discloses that explicitly: `Y2-5 CAGR: X% (trailing 3y, within consensus ceiling)`).
366	
367	Bear and bull formulas operate on the **capped** `base_cagr`, not on the raw trailing CAGR. This preserves bear/base/bull axis independence (each scenario applies its own multiplier on a common anchor) while preventing the entire range from inheriting a trough-extrapolation.
368	
369	| Scenario | Y2–Y5 CAGR | Narrative |
370	|---|---|---|
371	| Bear | `max(base_cagr × 0.50, 0.02)` | Secular deceleration; growth halves and floors at GDP |
372	| Base | `base_cagr` (capped per above) | Trailing 3y or consensus/fallback ceiling, whichever is lower |
373	| Bull | `base_cagr × 1.20 + 0.02` | Operating leverage extends runway; 200 bps uplift on the capped base |
374	
375	Project: `fcf_y_n = fcf_y_(n−1) × (1 + cagr)` for n = 2, 3, 4, 5.
376	
377	When `cap_applied = true`, the OUTPUT block's Base scenario line must surface the cap explicitly (`Y2-5 CAGR: X.X% (capped from trailing Y.Y% by <cap_source>)`); silent caps are forbidden. The `growth_rate` JSON block (see OUTPUT) records all five fields (trailing rate, consensus value, fallback ceiling, applied rate, cap source) every run — even when the cap does not fire — so the audit trail is symmetric.
378	
379	**Step 4 — Terminal value (Gordon Growth) per scenario.**
380	
381	| Scenario | Terminal growth `g` | WACC | Narrative |
382	|---|---|---|---|
383	| Bear | 1.5% | `base_wacc + 1.0pp` | Recession tail; cost of capital widens |
384	| Base | 2.5% | `base_wacc` | Long-run growth normalises to GDP+; current rates |
385	| Bull | 3.5% | `base_wacc − 1.0pp` | Above-GDP terminal; rate-easing tailwind |
386	
387	Terminal value at end of Year 5: `TV = fcf_y5 × (1 + g) / (wacc − g)`. Require `wacc > g` — if violated (sensitivity edge), narrow the WACC adjustment for that scenario and state the override in the output.
388	
389	**Step 5 — Discount to present.**
390	
391	For each scenario, the enterprise value is the sum of discounted explicit FCFs and the discounted terminal value:
392	
393	```
394	EV = Σ_{n=1..5} fcf_y_n / (1 + wacc)^n  +  TV / (1 + wacc)^5
395	```
396	
397	**Step 6 — Equity bridge and per-share intrinsic value.**
398	
399	- `net_debt = years[0].total_debt − years[0].cash`
```

652	
653	When no playbook is present: `"playbook": {"loaded": false, "path": null, "last_updated": null, "overrides_applied": [], "failure_modes": []}`.
654	
655	`overrides_applied` lists exactly the frontmatter fields that were consumed (i.e., had a non-null value and modified a DCF input or output). It does not list fields that were present but had no effect (e.g. `failure_modes` that were only displayed, or `last_updated`). Always emitted — even when `loaded: false` — so replay diffing can detect a missing playbook.
656	
657	The `engagement_modifier` block is emitted only when `--engagement-modifier` was passed. When the flag is absent, omit the block entirely (FR6 / spike-decision). When the flag is present but the modifier was not applied (any non-`applied` status), populate `status` and `status_reason` and emit the remaining fields as `null` — preserving the schema shape for replay diffing. When `status == "applied"`, all populated fields are required (NFR3). `kpi_map_schema_version` mirrors the top-level field of `skills/_shared/engagement_kpi_map.json` at run time per Task 8 ADR D5. `clamped_from` is non-null only when the output-cap clamp fired (Step 6b); otherwise `null`. `revision` and `agreement` are populated by GATHER Step 5b (Yahoo `/analysis/` EPS Trend fetch); when the fetch fails or parsing returns no values, `revision.revision_pct` and `revision.direction` are `null` and `agreement` is `null` (legacy fall-through — modifier still applies per C3/C4). `agreement == false` triggers the `direction_disagreement` status, in which case `base_anchor_multiplier = 1.00` (modifier suppressed per spike-decision guardrail).
658	
659	`fcf_ttm`, `fcf_margin_ttm`, and `fcf_cagr_3y` are the **SBC-stripped (clean)** values per ABA-110 — they anchor the DCF. `fcf_ttm_reported`, `fcf_margin_ttm_reported`, and `sbc_ttm` are audit-only fields exposing the pre-strip numbers so consumers can reconcile against yfinance's `free_cash_flow` and `stock_based_compensation` directly. The two pairs must satisfy `fcf_ttm == fcf_ttm_reported − sbc_ttm` and `fcf_margin_ttm == fcf_ttm / revenue_ttm` (within float tolerance).
660	
661	`growth_rate` is emitted every run per ABA-111. `trailing_3y_cagr` mirrors `fcf_cagr_3y` (the raw clean-FCF trailing CAGR, pre-cap). `consensus_5y_growth` is the value pulled from `get_estimates(ticker).eps_growth_5y` — `null` when yfinance has no long-term consensus. `fallback_ceiling` is the hard-coded 18% ceiling used when consensus is null. `applied_base_cagr` is `min(trailing_3y_cagr, ceiling)` — the value that drove the base scenario's Y2–Y5 projection (and the anchor that bear/bull scale off). `cap_applied` is `true` when `applied_base_cagr < trailing_3y_cagr`; `cap_source` is `consensus_5y_growth` / `fallback_ceiling` / `null` respectively. When the cap does not fire (trailing already at or below ceiling), `applied_base_cagr == trailing_3y_cagr` and `cap_source == null`.
662	
663	`intrinsic_value_range` is a flat extract of `scenarios.{bear,base,bull}.intrinsic_value_per_share` for UI convenience — must reconcile cell-for-cell with the scenarios block.
664	
665	Rows in `sensitivity_table.intrinsic_value_per_share` are indexed by `wacc_axis` (row 0 = lowest WACC); columns by `terminal_growth_axis` (col 0 = lowest g). `base_cell` names the index that should match `scenarios.base.intrinsic_value_per_share`. Suppressed cells (where `wacc ≤ g`) are emitted as `null`, not numbers.
666

668	
669	```

140	### `/stock-portfolio` skill
141	**Problem:** For the operator, we believe the current per-ticker pattern is causing portfolio-level questions ("which of my seven is most overvalued right now?") to require manual aggregation — which means they don't happen.
142	**Success criterion:** `/stock-portfolio` runs the screen + signal (and reads latest cached model) on all seven covered tickers, produces a ranked table: ticker / current price / our IV / margin of safety / verdict / last updated. Operator can answer "where should I add" in one invocation.
143	**Appetite:** ~1 week. v1 reads from `reports/`; does not re-run model every time.
144	**Theme:** differentiator (portfolio)
145	
146	### ABA-115 Glidepath + scenario fan visuals
147	**Problem:** For the operator, we believe text-only model outputs are causing the future trajectory of a company to be hard to internalise. Visuals would shorten the path from "I read the IV" to "I see what it implies."
148	**Success criterion:** `/stock-model` report includes a glidepath visual (revenue/FCF projection) and scenario fan (bear/base/bull); rendered in UI Model tab.
149	**Appetite:** ~1 week.
150	**Theme:** differentiator (legibility)
151	
152	## Next
153	
154	### Write remaining playbooks: META, NVDA, AMZN, NFLX, ADYEN
155	**Problem:** Five of the seven covered tickers still run on generic defaults. The whole depth-over-breadth thesis is unrealised until all seven have playbooks.
156	**Theme:** differentiator
157	
158	### Benchmark overlay (SPY / QQQ comparator)
159	**Problem:** For the operator, we believe per-ticker IVs don't answer the actual question — "should I buy this or just buy the index?" Without a benchmark layer, the pack can't help with opportunity-cost decisions, which is the second-most-common reason it's used.
160	**Theme:** differentiator (portfolio)
161	**Notes:** Likely lives as an addition to `/stock-portfolio` rather than a separate skill — column for "implied annualised return vs QQQ over [horizon]."
162	
163	### M2.5 data gaps — ABA-67, ABA-68, ABA-69, ABA-75
164	**Problem:** Bookings/backlog (capital-equipment cos), EPS revisions, next-earnings-date, and EDGAR SBC fallback (for non-US filers like ADYEN) are missing. `/stock-timing` and `/stock-model` quality is capped until these land.
165	**Theme:** table-stakes
166	
167	### ABA-65 Segment-revenue trend as growth modifier
168	**Problem:** For tickers where blended growth hides segment divergence (AWS in AMZN, Cloud in GOOG, DC in NVDA), blended NTM estimates produce wrong growth inputs.
169	**Theme:** differentiator
170	
171	### M6 Router — ABA-36/37/38/39/62/63
172	**Problem:** Manual chaining of Screen → Signal → Model works but creates friction; the router is how the pack becomes a daily tool rather than a per-decision tool.
173	**Theme:** table-stakes
174	**Notes:** Kept per operator decision — chain orchestration is core UX even though it's six issues.
175	
176	### UI Model tab + sensitivity table — ABA-42, ABA-43
177	**Problem:** Model outputs need a structured place to live; sensitivity is best read interactively.
178	**Theme:** differentiator (legibility)
179	
180	## Later
181	
182	### AlphaSpread reconciliation checklist (doc, not build)
183	**Hypothesis:** A written checklist for reconciling our IV against AlphaSpread's IV — where to look for the largest deltas (WACC, terminal growth, FCF base, capex schedule), how to decide which is right, when to update our model vs note an unreconciled disagreement — would capture most of the value of a build, with none of the brittleness.
184	**Theme:** differentiator
185	**Notes:** Per operator: ship as `docs/operations/reconciliation-checklist.md` when it's the next-most-valuable thing. No build slot.
186	
187	### UI polish — ABA-40, ABA-41, ABA-44, ABA-45, ABA-46
188	**Hypothesis:** Once Model tab + visuals are landed, the Signal / Timing / Summary tabs, verdict badges, and PDF export round out the UI. Lower urgency than the differentiator work above.
189	**Theme:** incremental
190	
191	### ABA-64 Playwright e2e suite
192	**Hypothesis:** Worth doing once the UI surface is stable — running it earlier means rewriting tests every cycle.
193	**Theme:** tech foundation
194	
195	### ABA-107 / ABA-108 KPI coverage page + ticker-add helper
196	**Hypothesis:** Useful as the pack matures, low marginal value while only seven tickers are covered.
197	**Theme:** incremental
198	
199	### Spikes / ADR / drift live / parity — ABA-105, ABA-103, ABA-109, ABA-113, ABA-95

343	The UI reads this file directly. Skills write valid JSON only — no prose in the JSON output.
344	
345	---
346	
347	## UI Layer
348	
349	**Stack:** Vite + React. No backend. Reads report JSON from `reports/` directory (loaded via `fetch` in development, or bundled in future if needed).
350	
351	**Report viewer behaviour:**
352	- Opens the most recently written report JSON by default
353	- Navigation tabs: Screen | Signal | Model | Timing | Summary
354	- Each tab renders the corresponding skill output with charts and colour-coded verdicts
355	- Sensitivity table in the Model tab is an interactive grid (editable WACC/terminal growth inputs that recalculate on the fly)
356	- Verdict badges: green (BUY / PASS / ACT NOW), amber (WATCH / CONDITIONAL / WAIT FOR CATALYST), red (CAUTION / SKIP / WAIT FOR BETTER ENTRY)
357	- Export button: saves current view as a PDF-ready print layout
358	
359	**Development:**
360	```bash
361	cd ui && npm install
362	npm run dev       # localhost:5173
363	npm run build     # dist/ for static serving
364	```
365	
366	**Skills open the report** by writing the JSON then printing a file path. The user opens it manually or via a browser-open command if available.
367	
368	---
369	
370	## Commands
371	
372	```bash
373	# Skill invocation (Claude Code slash commands)
374	/stock-equity NVDA                          # Full chain
375	/stock-equity Anthropic -- pre-profit AI    # Full chain with hint
376	/stock-screen AAPL, NVDA, META, RDDT, CRWV  # Screen only
377	/stock-signal Meta Platforms                 # Signal only
378	/stock-model Meta -- standard DCF           # Model (requires signal in context)
379	/stock-timing NVDA                           # Timing overlay
380	
381	# UI development
382	cd ui && npm run dev
383	cd ui && npm run build
384	
385	# MCP server startup (for testing)
386	cd mcp/yf && python server.py
387	cd mcp/edgar && python server.py
388	```
389	
390	---
391	
392	## Testing Strategy
393	
394	**Methodology validation (primary):** Run each skill against three known reference tickers and manually verify the output matches the expected methodology:
395	- NVDA — established profitable tech, AI infrastructure, large capex
396	- META — established profitable tech, incumbent with AI optionality
397	- RDDT — emerging/pre-profit, recent IPO, limited FCF history
398	
399	**MCP integration tests:** Unit test each tool function against live API responses with known tickers. Snapshot the response schema so regressions in the API structure are caught.
400	
401	**Signal contract test:** Run `/stock-signal NVDA`, capture the output block, confirm all required fields are present and correctly typed before invoking `/stock-model NVDA`.
402	
403	**SBC stripping test:** For a ticker with a known large SBC line (e.g. Salesforce CRM), verify that clean EPS diverges from reported EPS by the expected amount.
404	
405	**UI smoke test:** After any report JSON is written, confirm the UI renders without console errors and all tabs display data.
406	
407	**Coverage backtesting (future, ABA-NEXT):** Quarterly historical replays of `/stock-signal` and `/stock-model` against the supported tickers (`COVERAGE.md`). Grade IV-range coverage of subsequent price action, verdict accuracy, and dominant-driver stability across time. Feeds methodology drift back into the skills per `CHARTER.md` → *Operating Principle* (5).
408	
409	No automated test runner is mandated. Tests are skill-invocation runs with expected output documented in `tests/reference/`.
410	
411	---
412	
413	## GARP Threshold Calibration
414	
415	### Background
416	
417	`/stock-signal` applies GARP (Growth at a Reasonable Price) scoring to classify tickers as BUY / WATCH / CAUTION. PEG ratio (P/E ÷ 5-year EPS CAGR) is the primary lens for ESTABLISHED companies. A single universal threshold band (WATCH ≤ 2.0) systematically under-rates:
418	
419	- **Mega-cap INCUMBENT tech** (GOOG, AMZN, META): structural growth ceiling from $500B+ revenue base; market correctly prices in earnings durability. PEG 2–4× is normal for healthy fundamentals.
420	- **Infrastructure monopolies** (ASML EUV, NVDA GPU): pricing power + captive customer base sustains premium multiples across the capital-expenditure cycle. Historical PEG 2–5×.
421	- **Premium subscription / streaming** (NFLX): pricing leverage + low churn + content moat. Historical PEG 1.5–3.5× for profitable names.
422	- **Regional quality compounders without AI primary thesis** (ADYEN.AS): European fintech moat trading at slight premium to small-cap GARP baseline.
423	
424	**Decision (ABA-132):** Segment PEG thresholds by `ai_layer` for ESTABLISHED profit stage. Market-cap buckets were considered as an overlay but rejected for v1: `ai_layer` already captures the load-bearing structural differences (monopoly vs. mega-cap compounder vs. quality subscriber), and a second dimension doubles the table without clear added precision. Universal bands are preserved as the fallback for unknown / unclassified profiles.
```

121	def get_financials(ticker: str, period: str = "annual") -> dict:
122	    """Return multi-year income statement, balance sheet, and cash flow data.
123	
124	    Pulls from yfinance annual statements. yfinance returns 5 columns but the
125	    oldest column is NaN on all tested tickers; it is dropped automatically.
126	
127	    Args:
128	        ticker: Stock ticker symbol.
129	        period: Must be "annual". Other values raise ValueError.
130	
131	    Returns:
132	        {
133	            "ticker": str,
134	            "period": str,
135	            "years": [  # newest first, up to 4 years
136	                {
137	                    "fiscal_year": "YYYY-MM-DD",
138	                    "revenue": float | None,
139	                    "gross_profit": float | None,
140	                    "cost_of_revenue": float | None,
141	                    "operating_income": float | None,
142	                    "net_income": float | None,
143	                    "free_cash_flow": float | None,
144	                    "operating_cash_flow": float | None,
145	                    "capital_expenditures": float | None,
146	                    "stock_based_compensation": float | None,
147	                    "total_debt": float | None,
148	                    "long_term_debt": float | None,
149	                    "cash": float | None,
150	                    "total_assets": float | None,
151	                    "current_assets": float | None,
152	                    "current_liabilities": float | None,
153	                    "intangible_assets": float | None,
154	                    "shares_outstanding_diluted": float | None,
155	                }
156	            ]
157	        }
158	
159	    Notes on yfinance row mapping:
160	        - intangible_assets uses "Goodwill And Other Intangible Assets" (goodwill + other intangibles).
161	          Falls back to summing "Goodwill" + "Other Intangible Assets" when the combined row is absent.
162	        - shares_outstanding_diluted uses "Diluted Average Shares" from the income statement (annual avg).
163	        - capital_expenditures is reported negative by yfinance; we return the raw signed value.
164	    """
165	    if period != "annual":
166	        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported period {period!r}. Only 'annual' is supported in v1.")
167	
168	    start = time.monotonic()
169	    t = yf.Ticker(ticker)
170	    fin = t.financials
171	    cf = t.cashflow
172	    bs = t.balance_sheet
173	    print(f"[yf] get_financials({ticker}) → {time.monotonic() - start:.2f}s", file=sys.stderr)
174	
175	    if fin is None or fin.empty:
176	        raise YFNoDataError(
177	            f"yfinance returned no financial statements for {ticker}. "
178	            "Ticker may be delisted or mistyped."
179	        )
180	
181	    # Drop columns where every value is NaN (the phantom 5th year)
182	    valid_cols = [c for c in fin.columns if not fin[c].isna().all()]
183	
184	    years = []
185	    for col in sorted(valid_cols, reverse=True):  # newest first
186	        fy = col.strftime("%Y-%m-%d") if hasattr(col, "strftime") else str(col)[:10]
187	        # Intangibles: prefer the combined "Goodwill And Other Intangible Assets" row;
188	        # fall back to summing "Goodwill" + "Other Intangible Assets" when only those exist.
189	        if bs is not None:
190	            intangibles = _row(bs, "Goodwill And Other Intangible Assets", col)
191	            if intangibles is None:
192	                gw = _row(bs, "Goodwill", col)
193	                oi = _row(bs, "Other Intangible Assets", col)
194	                intangibles = (gw or 0) + (oi or 0) if (gw is not None or oi is not None) else None
195	        else:
196	            intangibles = None
197	
198	        year_data = {
199	            "fiscal_year": fy,
200	            "revenue": _row(fin, "Total Revenue", col),
201	            "gross_profit": _row(fin, "Gross Profit", col),
202	            "cost_of_revenue": _row(fin, "Cost Of Revenue", col),
203	            "operating_income": _row(fin, "Operating Income", col),
204	            "net_income": _row(fin, "Net Income", col),
205	            "free_cash_flow": _row(cf, "Free Cash Flow", col) if cf is not None else None,
206	            "operating_cash_flow": _row(cf, "Operating Cash Flow", col) if cf is not None else None,
207	            "capital_expenditures": _row(cf, "Capital Expenditure", col) if cf is not None else None,
208	            "stock_based_compensation": _row(cf, "Stock Based Compensation", col) if cf is not None else None,
209	            "total_debt": _row(bs, "Total Debt", col) if bs is not None else None,
210	            "long_term_debt": _row(bs, "Long Term Debt", col) if bs is not None else None,
211	            "cash": _row(bs, "Cash And Cash Equivalents", col) if bs is not None else None,
212	            "total_assets": _row(bs, "Total Assets", col) if bs is not None else None,
213	            "current_assets": _row(bs, "Current Assets", col) if bs is not None else None,
214	            "current_liabilities": _row(bs, "Current Liabilities", col) if bs is not None else None,
215	            "intangible_assets": intangibles,
216	            "shares_outstanding_diluted": _row(fin, "Diluted Average Shares", col),
217	        }
218	        # Drop years where every financial field is None (partial NaN columns)
219	        if any(v is not None for k, v in year_data.items() if k != "fiscal_year"):
220	            years.append(year_data)
221	
222	    return {"ticker": ticker, "period": period, "years": years}
223	
224	
```

This is the meta-skill that governs how all other skills are discovered and invoked.\n---\n\n# Using Agent Skills\n\n## Overview\n\nAgent Skills is a collection of engineering workflow skills organized by development phase. Each skill encodes a specific process that senior engineers follow. This meta-skill helps you discover and apply the right skill for your current task.\n\n## Skill Discovery\n\nWhen a task arrives, identify the development phase and apply the corresponding skill:\n\n```\nTask arrives\n    │\n    ├── Vague idea/need refinement? ──→ idea-refine\n    ├── New project/feature/change? ──→ spec-driven-development\n    ├── Have a spec, need tasks? ──────→ planning-and-task-breakdown\n    ├── Implementing code? ────────────→ incremental-implementation\n    │   ├── UI work? ─────────────────→ frontend-ui-engineering\n    │   ├── API work? ────────────────→ api-and-interface-design\n    │   ├── Need better context? ─────→ context-engineering\n    │   └── Need doc-verified code? ───→ source-driven-development\n    ├── Writing/running tests? ────────→ test-driven-development\n    │   └── Browser-based? ───────────→ browser-testing-with-devtools\n    ├── Something broke? ──────────────→ debugging-and-error-recovery\n    ├── Reviewing code? ───────────────→ code-review-and-quality\n    │   ├── Security concerns? ───────→ security-and-hardening\n    │   └── Performance concerns? ────→ performance-optimization\n    ├── Committing/branching? ─────────→ git-workflow-and-versioning\n    ├── CI/CD pipeline work? ──────────→ ci-cd-and-automation\n    ├── Writing docs/ADRs? ───────────→ documentation-and-adrs\n    └── Deploying/launching? ─────────→ shipping-and-launch\n```\n\n## Core Operating Behaviors\n\nThese behaviors apply at all times, across all skills. They are non-negotiable.\n\n### 1. Surface Assumptions\n\nBefore implementing anything non-trivial, explicitly state your assumptions:\n\n```\nASSUMPTIONS I'M MAKING:\n1. [assumption about requirements]\n2. [assumption about architecture]\n3. [assumption about scope]\n→ Correct me now or I'll proceed with these.\n```\n\nDon't silently fill in ambiguous requirements. The most common failure mode is making wrong assumptions and running with them unchecked. Surface uncertainty early — it's cheaper than rework.\n\n### 2. Manage Confusion Actively\n\nWhen you encounter inconsistencies, conflicting requirements, or unclear specifications:\n\n1. **STOP.** Do not proceed with a guess.\n2. Name the specific confusion.\n3. skills/stock-model/SKILL.md:3:description: DCF/valuation model for a tech stock. Invoked as `/stock-model TICKER [--confirm] [--pre-profit] [--wacc=X.X]`. Reads MODEL_READY from the upstream `/stock-signal` (conversation context preferred, same-day `reports/TICKER_YYYYMMDD.json` fallback) and branches: YES → run DCF and emit a bear/base/bull intrinsic-value range; CONDITIONAL → halt and surface `condition` (or proceed when `--confirm` is passed); NO → refuse and surface `qualitative_note`. ESTABLISHED profile uses two-stage DCF (5y FCF + Gordon terminal). EMERGING profile (or any ticker invoked with `--pre-profit`) uses revenue-multiple exit + FCF inflection + SBC dilution schedule. For watchlist tickers with a `playbooks/TICKER.md`, loads ticker-specific WACC, growth ceiling, narratives, failure modes, and confidence cap before the DCF runs. Use whenever the user asks for an intrinsic value, fair-value range, DCF, or "what's it worth" on a ticker.
skills/stock-model/SKILL.md:8:**Command:** `/stock-model TICKER [--confirm] [--pre-profit]`
skills/stock-model/SKILL.md:9:**Purpose:** Run a DCF for a tech stock and emit a bear/base/bull intrinsic-value range, after enforcing the upstream-context contract from `/stock-signal`. ESTABLISHED → two-stage DCF (5y FCF + Gordon terminal). EMERGING (or any ticker forced with `--pre-profit`) → revenue-multiple exit + FCF inflection year + SBC dilution schedule.
skills/stock-model/SKILL.md:16:- **Command:** `/stock-model TICKER [--confirm] [--pre-profit]`
skills/stock-model/SKILL.md:17:- **Purpose:** Gate on the upstream Signal, then compute a DCF under three demonstrably-different scenario sets. ESTABLISHED profile → two-stage DCF (5y explicit FCF + Gordon terminal). EMERGING profile (or any ticker forced with `--pre-profit`) → revenue-multiple exit + FCF inflection + SBC dilution. Output a per-share intrinsic-value range (bear/base/bull) with each scenario's assumptions and narrative fully disclosed, and merge into `reports/TICKER_YYYYMMDD.json` under `stages.model`.
skills/stock-model/SKILL.md:29:- `NO` — Signal has already ruled the DCF out (pre-profit, qualitative FAIL, or hard CAUTION). Refuse and point back to Signal.
skills/stock-model/SKILL.md:33:v1.2 (ABA-31) lands the standard two-stage DCF for ESTABLISHED profile. v1.3 (ABA-34) lands the pre-profit variant — revenue-multiple exit, explicit FCF inflection year per scenario, and SBC-driven dilution schedule. The `--pre-profit` flag forces the pre-profit variant even on an ESTABLISHED upstream classification (useful for transition-year tickers like RDDT whose recent profitability does not yet support a stable terminal-value calculation). v1.7 (ABA-110) strips SBC from the FCF base on both paths — `fcf_ttm`, `fcf_margin_ttm`, and `fcf_cagr_3y` are now clean (SBC-stripped) on the ESTABLISHED path, and `margin_ttm` is clean on the pre-profit path. This brings Model into methodology consistency with Signal Step 5, which has always stripped SBC from EPS. v1.8 (ABA-111) caps the Y2–Y5 CAGR against `get_estimates.eps_growth_5y` (or an 18% fallback on ESTABLISHED / 35% on pre-profit) — trailing CAGR off a depressed base year is a useful floor signal but a poor central case; the cap prevents the entire scenario range from inheriting a trough-extrapolation. Both v1.7 and v1.8 are pre-requisites for any `/stock-model` report to be treated as decision-grade.
skills/stock-model/SKILL.md:41:1. Parse the ticker from the command argument. Uppercase it (e.g. `nvda` → `NVDA`). If blank, refuse immediately with: "Usage: `/stock-model TICKER [--confirm] [--pre-profit]`".
skills/stock-model/SKILL.md:43:3. Parse the optional `--pre-profit` flag. Its effect is to force the pre-profit variant in ROUTE regardless of the upstream `profit_stage`. Accepted spellings: `--pre-profit`, `-- pre-profit` (separator tolerant). When set, record `route_override = "pre-profit"` for the OUTPUT block.
skills/stock-model/SKILL.md:44:4. Parse the optional `--engagement-modifier` flag. **Default: off.** When absent, the engagement-modifier GATHER sub-step is skipped entirely and the `engagement_modifier` JSON block is omitted (FR6 / spike-decision: advisory + flag-gated default-off until n≥24 forward samples accumulate; see `docs/design-docs/engagement-kpi-enrichment/spike-decision.md`). When present, the sub-step runs per the rules in **GATHER — Engagement modifier** below (ESTABLISHED path only — EMERGING path always skips per FR6). Accepted spellings: `--engagement-modifier`, `-- engagement-modifier` (separator tolerant).

700	**Manual Input Protocol fires (always grouped — one paste-in):**
701	
702	- **Base WACC** — same rule as ESTABLISHED; yfinance has no risk-free-rate / beta surface. Never default.
703	- **Exit EV/Revenue multiple — base case.** Present the comp list and ask the user to confirm or override the base multiple. Bear is `base × 0.6`, bull is `base × 1.4` (state these scenario axes; do not ask separately).
704	- **Terminal clean FCF margin target.** Industry-anchored, **SBC-stripped** (per ABA-110). When playbook provides `terminal_margin`, present it as a pre-filled value — `Terminal clean FCF margin: X.X% (from playbook — confirm or override:)`. Otherwise ask the user for the steady-state *clean* FCF margin the company is expected to achieve by Year 5 under the base case — i.e. FCF after stock-based compensation has been deducted, not the industry-reported number. State the distinction explicitly in the prompt so users do not anchor on SBC-included margins. Bear is `target − 5 pp`, bull is `target + 5 pp`. Never default.
705	- **SBC dilution rate (annual %).** Pre-fill with the trailing 3y dilution rate derived from `shares_outstanding_diluted` history: `dilution_3y = (years[0].shares / years[3].shares) ^ (1/3) − 1`. Surface this number and the historical share-count series, and ask the user to confirm or override. Bear is `confirmed × 1.5`, bull is `confirmed × 0.5` (dilution accelerates in bear, decelerates in bull) — state these axes; do not ask separately.
706	
707	Manual inputs are tagged `source: "user_paste"`; `meta.confidence` caps at MEDIUM. If the user accepts every pre-filled value with no override, still record `source: "user_confirmed"` — the value is load-bearing on the output.
708	
709	If `get_financials` returns null `stock_based_compensation` for the latest year: refuse with `Pre-profit variant requires SBC data; yf MCP returned null. Re-run with --confirm only after SBC is verified manually.` Do not proceed without SBC — the dilution schedule is a first-class output.
710	
711	### COMPUTE — Pre-profit (revenue-multiple exit + FCF inflection)
712	
713	Execute in order. The math anchors on revenue (not FCF) because pre-profit companies do not yet have a stable FCF base.
714	
715	**Step 1 — Revenue trajectory per scenario (sanity-capped per ABA-111).**
716	
717	- `rev_cagr_3y = (years[0].revenue / years[3].revenue) ^ (1/3) − 1` (use shorter history with the same degraded formulations as ESTABLISHED Step 1 if 4y is unavailable).
718	- Apply the same growth-ceiling pattern as ESTABLISHED Step 3, with a higher fallback because pre-profit tech routinely grows revenue 30–40% sustainably:
719	
720	  ```
721	  consensus_rev_growth = derived from get_estimates if available  # currently null on yf — placeholder for when an EDGAR/sell-side revenue-growth estimate lands
722	  fallback_ceiling     = 0.35                                      # 35% — pre-profit revenue ceiling
723	  playbook_ceiling     = playbook.growth_ceiling if playbook.loaded else None   # ABA-112: playbook override
724	
725	  # Priority: consensus > playbook ceiling > generic fallback
726	  ceiling              = (consensus_rev_growth if consensus_rev_growth is not None
727	                         else (playbook_ceiling if playbook_ceiling is not None
728	                         else fallback_ceiling))
729	  base_rev_cagr        = min(rev_cagr_3y, ceiling)
730	  rev_cap_applied      = (base_rev_cagr < rev_cagr_3y)
731	  rev_cap_source       = ("consensus_rev_growth" if (consensus_rev_growth is not None and rev_cap_applied)
732	                         else "playbook"         if (playbook_ceiling is not None and rev_cap_applied)
733	                         else "fallback_ceiling" if rev_cap_applied
734	                         else None)
735	  ```
736	
737	- Year-1 revenue is anchored on NTM consensus, perturbed per scenario. Y2–Y5 CAGRs operate on the **capped** `base_rev_cagr`:
738	
739	| Scenario | Y1 revenue | Y2–Y5 CAGR |
740	|---|---|---|
741	| Bear | `ntm_revenue × 0.85` | `max(base_rev_cagr × 0.50, 0.05)` |
742	| Base | `ntm_revenue` | `base_rev_cagr` (capped) |
743	| Bull | `ntm_revenue × 1.15` | `base_rev_cagr × 1.15 + 0.03` |
744	
745	Project `revenue_y_n = revenue_y_(n−1) × (1 + cagr)` for n = 2..5. When `rev_cap_applied = true`, the OUTPUT block's Base scenario revenue-CAGR figure must surface the cap explicitly (same disclosure pattern as ESTABLISHED), and the `growth_rate` JSON block (see OUTPUT) records the five fields every run.
746	
747	**Step 2 — FCF margin trajectory and inflection year.**
748	
749	- `sbc_ttm` = `years[0].stock_based_compensation` (already required by the GATHER refusal above — non-null by the time this step runs).
750	- `fcf_ttm_reported = years[0].free_cash_flow` (audit-trail only).
751	- `fcf_ttm_clean = fcf_ttm_reported − sbc_ttm` (SBC-stripped FCF — the cash-flow side of the trajectory).
752	- `margin_ttm_reported = fcf_ttm_reported / years[0].revenue` (audit-trail only; surfaced in OUTPUT side-by-side with clean).
753	- `margin_ttm = fcf_ttm_clean / years[0].revenue` (the **clean** SBC-stripped margin — this is the trajectory baseline, may be negative). The user-confirmed terminal margin in the GATHER MIP is a **clean FCF margin target** — surface this label explicitly in the prompt so users do not anchor on industry-reported (SBC-included) margins.
754	- Target Year-5 margin per scenario: `target − 5pp` (bear) / `target` (base) / `target + 5pp` (bull), where `target` is the user-confirmed terminal clean FCF margin.
755	- Linear interpolation across Years 1–5: `margin_y_n = margin_ttm + (target − margin_ttm) × (n / 5)`.
756	- `fcf_y_n = revenue_y_n × margin_y_n` (negative in early years if the company is still cash-burning).
757	- **FCF inflection year:** the smallest n ∈ {1..5} such that `fcf_y_n ≥ 0` AND `fcf_y_m ≥ 0` for all m ≥ n in {1..5} (i.e., first year of *sustained* positive FCF — not a one-off blip).
758	- If no scenario reaches positive FCF by Y5, name it explicitly: `inflection_year = "beyond Y5"`. Do **not** silently assume eventual inflection — surface it as a model limitation in the OUTPUT block.
759	
760	**Step 3 — Exit enterprise value.**
761	
762	| Scenario | Exit EV/Revenue multiple | Exit EV at end of Year 5 |
763	|---|---|---|
764	| Bear | `base_multiple × 0.6` | `revenue_y5 × bear_multiple` |
765	| Base | `base_multiple` (user-confirmed) | `revenue_y5 × base_multiple` |
766	| Bull | `base_multiple × 1.4` | `revenue_y5 × bull_multiple` |
767	
768	No Gordon-growth terminal — the exit-multiple is the terminal. State that explicitly in the OUTPUT block.
769	
770	**Step 4 — SBC dilution schedule.**
771	
772	- `dilution_rate` per scenario: `confirmed_rate × 1.5` (bear) / `confirmed_rate` (base) / `confirmed_rate × 0.5` (bull).
773	- `shares_y_n = shares_y_(n−1) × (1 + dilution_rate)` for n = 1..5, starting from `years[0].shares_outstanding_diluted`.
774	- The Year-5 diluted share count is the divisor for per-share IV — using today's share count overstates IV. This is the whole reason dilution is first-class.
775	- Also report `dilution_pct_5y = (shares_y5 / shares_y0) − 1` for the OUTPUT block.
776	
777	**Step 5 — Discount to present.**
778	
779	For each scenario:
780	
781	```
782	EV_today = Σ_{n=1..5} fcf_y_n / (1 + wacc)^n   +   exit_ev / (1 + wacc)^5
783	```
784	
785	The first sum includes negative FCFs in early years if the company is still burning cash — this is a real present-value drag and must be carried (do not floor at zero).
786	
787	**Step 6 — Equity bridge and per-share intrinsic value.**
788	
789	- `net_debt = years[0].total_debt − years[0].cash` (keep the sign; negative means net cash).
790	- `equity_value = EV_today − net_debt`
791	- `intrinsic_value_per_share = equity_value / shares_y5`  ← **Year-5 diluted shares**, not today's.
792	- `upside_pct = (intrinsic_value_per_share / currentPrice − 1) × 100`
793	
794	**Step 7 — Range integrity check.**
795	
796	Same rule as ESTABLISHED: require `bear_iv < base_iv < bull_iv`. If violated, stop, surface the three scenario input/output tables, and ask the user to widen the exit-multiple, margin-target, or dilution-rate deltas. Never silently re-order.
797	
798	**Step 8 — Sensitivity note.**
799	
800	For the pre-profit variant the dominant drivers are usually exit multiple and terminal margin (not WACC). Vary one assumption at a time around base:
801	
802	- Exit multiple ± 1.0× (absolute)
803	- Terminal FCF margin ± 300 bps
804	- Dilution rate ± 200 bps annual
805	- WACC ± 100 bps
806	
807	Report the largest-swing driver as a one-line note. The numbers shown are the percentage change in base IV.
808	
809	### THRESHOLD — Range vs price
810	
811	Identical to the ESTABLISHED path (MARGIN OF SAFETY / WITHIN BEAR–BASE / WITHIN BASE–BULL / PRICE EXCEEDS RANGE).
812

934	`growth_rate` on the pre-profit path is the **revenue** CAGR cap per ABA-111 (vs ESTABLISHED's FCF CAGR cap) — same five-field shape, but `trailing_3y_cagr` mirrors `rev_cagr_3y`, the `fallback_ceiling` is `0.35` (higher than ESTABLISHED's 0.18 because pre-profit tech routinely sustains 30–40% revenue growth), and `cap_source` reads `consensus_rev_growth` when an external revenue-growth estimate is available (currently always null on yf).

The user-confirmed `terminal_margin_target` is a clean FCF margin target (the MIP prompt states this explicitly).

The per-driver dominant-driver note in `sensitivity` remains the only sensitivity artefact for this path. `intrinsic_value_range` is a flat extract of `scenarios.{bear,base,bull}.intrinsic_value_per_share` — must reconcile.

940	The `engagement_modifier` block is **never emitted on the EMERGING path** regardless of the `--engagement-modifier` flag (FR6 — pre-profit revenue scaffolding has no incumbent KPI baseline). If the flag is passed alongside an EMERGING ticker, emit a one-line notice to the user: `--engagement-modifier ignored: EMERGING path (FR6)` and proceed without it.

947	```
948	
949	**Confidence rules (`meta.confidence`):**
950	
951	- HIGH: not reachable for pre-profit — by construction, the variant relies on user-confirmed comp multiples and margin targets, so confidence caps at MEDIUM.
952	- MEDIUM: comp set retrieved from web search with ≥3 matches; all manual inputs user-confirmed; range integrity passed first time; SBC data present.
953	- LOW: <3 comp matches, or SBC data partial, or range integrity required user re-input, or no scenario reaches FCF inflection by Y5.
954	
955	---
956	
957	## 4. Common rationalisations to pre-rebut
958	
959	| Rationalisation | Counter |
960	|---|---|
961	| "The user asked for a model, I should produce *some* valuation rather than refuse." | The gate exists precisely to prevent this. Producing a fabricated valuation is worse than refusing — it gives the user a confident-looking number with no methodology behind it. Refuse and instruct. |
962	| "I can see the ticker; I'll just run the Signal logic inline and then the Model logic." | Two skills, two responsibilities. Inlining Signal here duplicates methodology and bypasses the JSON merge contract that downstream consumers (UI, screen reports) depend on. Refuse and instruct. |
963	| "There's a `reports/TICKER_YYYYMMDD.json` with a `stages.signal` block — that should count as upstream context." | Accepted, but **only if dated today**. Same-day JSON is a valid upstream source; conversation context still wins when both are present. Reject any report file not dated today — stale Signal data is fabrication risk; instruct the user to re-run Signal. |
964	| "The SIGNAL OUTPUT block is for a different ticker but it has useful classification info." | Tickers are not fungible. A SIGNAL OUTPUT for META tells you nothing about NVDA's profit stage or AI layer. Refuse and instruct. |
965	| "MODEL_READY is CONDITIONAL but the condition looks minor — I'll proceed without `--confirm`." | The whole point of CONDITIONAL is to force an explicit user acknowledgement of the named risk before DCF inputs are committed. Surface the condition verbatim and halt. Do not pre-judge what "minor" means on the user's behalf. |
966	| "MODEL_READY is NO but the user clearly wants a number — I'll produce one with a caveat." | A NO from Signal is a hard refusal, not a soft warning. Pre-profit companies and qualitative-FAIL companies have no defensible DCF; producing one with a caveat gives the user a confident-looking number anchored to nothing. Refuse and point back to Signal. |
967	| "I'll paraphrase the `condition` / `qualitative_note` to make it punchier." | Surface them **verbatim**. The user needs to see the exact wording Signal produced — paraphrasing introduces drift between what Signal said and what Model reported, which breaks the audit trail. |
968	| "A DCF input is null — I'll assume a reasonable default (WACC 10%, terminal growth 3%, …)" | Forbidden for derivable inputs. Per the Manual Input Protocol (`skills/_shared/MANUAL_INPUT_PROTOCOL.md`), when any DCF input cannot be derived from MCP data the skill MUST ask the user via a grouped paste-in. Base WACC is always asked (yfinance has no risk-free-rate / beta / capital-structure surface). Per-scenario terminal-g (1.5/2.5/3.5) and per-scenario WACC adjustments are **not** defaults — they are scenario-narrative axes disclosed in the OUTPUT block. |
969	| "I'll save typing by applying the same growth haircut across bear/base/bull (e.g. base × 0.7 / 1.0 / 1.3)." | Forbidden. The acceptance contract is "demonstrably different assumption sets, not percentage haircuts of a single set." Each scenario must move on its own axes (Y1 anchor, Y2–5 CAGR, terminal g, WACC) with its own narrative. The skill's range-integrity check (Step 7) is the executable form of this rule. |
```

> AGENT

Research complete. Here's what I found and the decisions I'd want to flush out before planning.

## What's already in place

**UI shell (`ui/`)** is Vite + React 18, no chart libraries. Today only the **Screen** tab renders (`ui/src/components/ScreenReport.jsx`); `Signal | Model | Timing | Summary` are stubbed with *"not yet implemented"* (`App.jsx:64`). Reports are loaded eagerly via `import.meta.glob('../../../reports/*.json')` (`ui/src/lib/reports.js`).

**Model JSON schema** (per `skills/stock-model/SKILL.md:551–665`, confirmed against `reports/AMZN_20260518.json`):
- `stages.model.growth_rate.{trailing_3y_cagr, consensus_5y_growth, fallback_ceiling, applied_base_cagr, cap_applied, cap_source}` — **ABA-111 landed (commit 6ac005a)**
- `stages.model.fcf_margin_ttm` (clean) and `fcf_margin_ttm_reported` (reported) — **ABA-110 landed**
- `stages.model.scenarios.{bear,base,bull}.{y1_fcf, y2_5_cagr, terminal_growth, wacc, intrinsic_value_per_share, upside_pct, narrative}` — pre-existing
- `stages.model.fcf_ttm`, `fcf_cagr_3y`, `current_price`, `shares_diluted`, `ntm_revenue` available

**Playbook layer** (ABA-112, commit 51646d2) has landed; the issue's "wait for ABA-112" prereq is satisfied. Playbooks for ASML/GOOG/META exist; AMZN/NFLX/NVDA do not.

**Roadmap context** (`docs/roadmap.md:81,89`): ABA-115 is in *Now*; the UI Model tab (ABA-42) is in *Next*. So we are effectively building the Model tab as the host surface in this ticket.

## Data gaps and inconsistencies in the ticket

These are the things I'd want resolved before planning, not silently chosen:

1. **FCF-margin chart — scope says 3y historical, AC #3 points at TTM-only.** The issue body asks for *"3y historical reported vs clean FCF margin"*, but Acceptance Criterion #3 reads from `fcf_margin_ttm` / `fcf_margin_ttm_reported` — both single TTM scalars. The JSON has no `historical_fcf_margins[]` series today. To honour the body, the Model SKILL has to emit a new historical-margin series (the raw data exists — `years[y].free_cash_flow` and `stock_based_compensation` are inside the SKILL during COMPUTE; they're just not persisted). Either path is fine, but the choice expands or contracts scope.

2. **Glidepath Y1 growth is not explicit in the JSON.** Trailing-3y CAGR and Y2–Y5 CAGRs are; Y1 has to be back-derived per scenario as `y1_fcf / fcf_ttm − 1`. Fine to compute client-side, but worth naming so the chart's "Y1 step" matches what the SKILL printed.

3. **Scenario fan — units / axes are ambiguous.** "Y1→Y5 FCF projection" is per-entity (billions); "terminal-year discounted-to-PV markers" and "current price line" are per-share. Two reasonable resolutions: (a) put everything on a per-share basis (divide FCF by `shares_diluted`), or (b) dual axes with FCF on the left and per-share IV/price on the right. (a) is cleaner; (b) is more faithful to the literal scope wording. I'd prefer (a).

4. **Pre-profit (EMERGING) variant — AC #6 says "decide during design".** The pre-profit JSON has different field names (`y1_revenue`, `y2_5_rev_cagr`, `terminal_margin`, `dilution_pct_5y`, `sensitivity_table: null`). Only RDDT is currently covered as pre-profit. Defaulting to "scoped out for v1, add a one-liner in the UI when `method == "pre-profit..."`" seems right, but it's an explicit call.

## Build choices that aren't decided yet

- **Chart library or hand-rolled SVG.** Repo has no chart deps; 3 charts are small enough to hand-roll in React+SVG (~150–300 LOC each, full control over annotations like the cap callout). Adding `recharts` is ~60 KB gzipped and saves code at the cost of an extra dep + an existing-style mismatch (the rest of the UI is plain divs/CSS).
- **Model-tab host.** This ticket de-facto builds the first version of the Model tab (replaces the stub in `App.jsx:64`). It's worth deciding whether to render *only* the three visuals, or also the basics (IV range, range_vs_price, position sizing) — so the tab is usable on its own rather than three orphan charts.
- **Caption copy.** AC #5 wants one-sentence captions that surface the *thesis implication*, not the chart mechanics. These need to be written; the methodology footnote in the ticket is the source material.

## Suggested clarifying questions for the next step

I'd want to nail (1) the FCF-margin historical-series scope (extend JSON, or TTM-only), (2) the scenario-fan unit choice (per-share single axis vs dual axis), (3) chart library vs hand-rolled SVG, and (4) whether the Model tab in this PR carries only the three visuals or also the basic Model summary fields, before drafting a plan.

for each historical year. **Insertion point:** immediately after the CAGR calculation (line 316), persist the 3y per-year clean margins: `historical_fcf_margins = [{year: YYYY, clean: X.X%, reported: Y.Y%}, ...]` — the numerator (`clean_fcf_y / years[y].revenue`) is trivial.

# ABA-115 — Sliced Plan

## Dependency graph

```
A (SKILL schema change)  ──►  B (re-run 4 reports)  ──►  D, E, F (chart slices)
                                                              │
C (Model-tab shell)  ──────────────────────────────────────►  D/E/F (mount points)
                                                              │
                                                              └──►  G (polish + docs)
```

- **A blocks B** — re-runs need the new fields.
- **B blocks D/E/F** — charts need real `historical_fcf_margins` data; without it the FCF-trajectory chart cannot be visually verified.
- **C is independent of A/B** — the shell renders existing fields (`intrinsic_value_range`, `range_vs_price`, `position_sizing`) that already exist in the current AMZN/META/NVDA reports. **Land C first or in parallel with A**, then chart slices land into a working shell.
- **D/E/F are mutually independent** once C and B are merged — they can ship in any order or in parallel.

**Recommended re-ordering vs. the brief: do C before A** (or in parallel). Rationale: the shell is the lowest-risk slice, gives you a place to eyeball each `stages.model` field before touching the SKILL, and de-risks the visual-design conversation (typography, IV-header layout, badge placement) before chart code lands.

## Slices

- Pre-profit branch: when `stages.model.method` starts with `"pre-profit"`, render the same IV-range + position-sizing summary plus a single italic line: *"Visual breakdown is ESTABLISHED-only for v1."*
- New formatters live in `ui/src/lib/formatters.js`: `formatCurrency`, `formatPercent`, `formatBillions`, `formatPerShare`. Each handles `null`/`NaN` → `—`.
- New component file: `ui/src/components/ModelReport.jsx`.

**Gate to next slice:** all four tickers render correctly with current (un-extended) JSON. If anything is broken, fix before touching the SKILL.

### Slice A — SKILL schema change

- Caption.

**Verification**
- META: current-price line ($614) intersects between bear-IV ($444) and base-IV ($1155) markers, visually matching `range_vs_price = "WITHIN BEAR-BASE"`. Bear-band bottom ≥ 0; bull-band markers ordered correctly.

---

### Slice G — Captions, polish, docs
**Acceptance criteria**
- All three captions are one sentence, explain what the viewer should learn (not what the chart is).
- Styles in `ui/src/styles.css`: chart container, caption type style, axis/tick colours match existing card palette (`#777`, `#fafafa`, `#e5e5e5`).
- PR description flags ABA-42 as subsumed and links it; PR mentions v1.11 SKILL bump.
- `DESIGN.md` (or relevant doc) updated if it documents UI tabs.

**Verification**
- Manual visual review on all four tickers + at least one no-model ticker (e.g. ASML) — picker disables Model tab as before.
- Print/screenshot all three charts at 960px viewport.

## Risks (surface in issue body)

1. **Schema change drift** — re-runs might silently change `intrinsic_value_range` if a code path is touched beyond intent. Mitigation: Slice B compares to prior IVs.
2. **`shares_diluted` units** — the fan chart math depends on raw share count, not millions. AMZN report has 10.827B; getting the unit wrong yields per-share fan 1000× off. Spot-check Y1 FCF/share against base IV order of magnitude.
3. **Pre-profit fallback ambiguity** — RDDT's `fcf_margin_ttm` is real (0.31) so the margin chart *could* render; explicit decision: do not render visuals for any `method` starting with `"pre-profit"`, regardless of field presence.
4. **`import.meta.glob` eager** — adding chart-heavy components may bloat the bundle. Confirm dev server still hot-reloads at <1s.
5. **Cap annotation overlap** — when `growth_rate.cap_applied = true` and `cap_source = "consensus_5y_growth"` is null elsewhere, the glidepath footnote can become noisy. Pick one canonical phrasing.
6. **AMZN normalisation_note edge case** — AMZN's clean TTM margin is negative; the margin-trajectory chart needs to handle a sign flip between historical and projected without breaking axis scaling.

### Critical Files for Implementation
- /Users/anton/src/stock-review/skills/stock-model/SKILL.md
- /Users/anton/src/stock-review/ui/src/App.jsx
- /Users/anton/src/stock-review/ui/src/components/ScreenReport.jsx
- /Users/anton/src/stock-review/ui/src/lib/formatters.js
- /Users/anton/src/stock-review/ui/src/styles.css

## Approved Plan (edited by user):
# ABA-115 — `/stock-model` glidepath + scenario fan visuals

## Context

`/stock-model` now produces methodologically honest but stark-looking scenario outputs after ABA-110 (SBC strip) and ABA-111 (growth-rate cap) landed. Example: META 2026-05-17 — bear $444, base $1155, bull $2440 vs price $614. Reading the base case in isolation is misleading without the trajectory context — the cap mechanically applies a consensus ceiling in Y2 that looks like a cliff but is a smoothing artefact. The reader has to do the unpacking in their head.

The fix is three visuals on the Model tab, sourced entirely from `stages.model.*`:

1. **CAGR glidepath** — trailing-3y CAGR → Y1→Y5 projected CAGR for each scenario, annotated where the cap fires.
2. **FCF margin trajectory** — 3y historical clean vs reported margin + flat-line extension through Y1→Y5, surfacing the "base assumes no operating leverage" assumption.
3. **Scenario fan** — Y1→Y5 FCF/share for bear/base/bull, with per-share terminal IV markers and a current-price reference line.

The Model tab does not yet exist in the UI; this ticket also lands v1 of it (subsumes ABA-42).

## Decisions taken before planning

1. **Schema-extend the Model SKILL** to emit `stages.model.historical_fcf_margins[]` (3y per-year `{year, fcf_margin_clean, fcf_margin_reported}`). The UI gracefully falls back to TTM-only when the field is absent.
2. **Scenario fan uses a single per-share axis** — projected FCF is divided by `shares_diluted`; terminal IV markers and the current-price line live on the same axis.
3. **Hand-rolled React + SVG**, no chart dependency.
4. **Model tab includes basic summary** (IV-range, range_vs_price badge, position-sizing line) alongside the three visuals — subsumes ABA-42 v1; flag in PR.
5. **Pre-profit (EMERGING) scoped OUT for v1.** When `stages.model.method` starts with `"pre-profit"`, the tab shows summary only plus a one-line note that visuals are ESTABLISHED-only.

## Dependency graph

```
Slice 1 (Model-tab shell)        ─► land first; no SKILL changes
        │
        └─► mount point for D/E/F

Slice 2 (SKILL: historical_fcf_margins)  ──► Slice 3 (re-run covered tickers)
                                                        │
                                                        └─► Slices 4 / 5 / 6 (charts, independently parallel)
                                                                          │
                                                                          └─► Slice 7 (captions, styles, docs)
```

- Slice 1 is independent of the SKILL change — the summary fields it consumes already exist in current reports.
- Slice 2 must land before Slice 3 (re-runs need new fields).
- Slices 4–6 are mutually independent once Slice 3 lands; can ship serially or in parallel.
- Slice 7 closes out.

## Slices

### Slice 1 — Model-tab shell (no SKILL changes)
**Files:** `ui/src/App.jsx`, new `ui/src/components/ModelReport.jsx`, `ui/src/lib/formatters.js`, `ui/src/styles.css`.

**Reuse:** `verdictClass()` from `formatters.js` for the `range_vs_price` badge; existing `.card`, `.badge`, `.tab` styles; `ScreenReport.jsx` as the shape pattern.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- `App.jsx` line 64 stub replaced for `tab === 'Model'`.
- ESTABLISHED branch renders: header row (method, profit_stage, base WACC, NTM revenue, clean FCF margin TTM); IV-range strip showing bear/base/bull `intrinsic_value_per_share` and `current_price`; `range_vs_price` badge using existing verdict palette; position-sizing band + one-line `rationale`.
- Pre-profit branch renders summary fields and an italic line: *"Visual breakdown is ESTABLISHED-only for v1."*
- New formatters added: `formatCurrency`, `formatPercent`, `formatBillions`, `formatPerShare`. Each returns `'—'` for `null`/`undefined`/`NaN`.
- Selecting a report without `stages.model` (e.g. ASML_20260517) keeps the Model tab disabled, same as today.

**Verification:** `npm run dev` from `ui/`. Cycle picker through AMZN_20260518, META_20260517, NVDA_20260514, RDDT_20260513. All four render without console errors. RDDT shows the pre-profit note. ASML/GOOG show the Model tab disabled.

**Gate to Slice 2:** all four ticker reports render the shell. If anything breaks, fix before touching the SKILL.

The margin trajectory chart should plot the *unmodified* clean TTM (the documented value), not the normalised one — the chart's purpose is to surface the gap that justified normalisation, not to hide it.
4. **Cap-annotation phrasing** — `cap_source` can be `consensus_5y_growth | playbook | fallback_ceiling | null`. Pick one canonical sentence template and reuse it.
5. **Vite glob bundle size** — `import.meta.glob('../../../reports/*.json', { eager: true })` already loads every report on every page. Three SVG components add little, but watch dev hot-reload latency stays <1s.
